\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}_{\leq k}(X_{u},X_{\leq k-1},X_{k})\,\big|\,X_{u}=x_{u},X_{\leq k-1}=x_{\leq k-1}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{\leq k-1}^{2}(x).

By imposing the
first assumption
on
C_{0}
that

C_{0}\geq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log\Big(\frac{1}{c^{*}\min_{j\in[q]}\pi(j)}\Big),

it follows from (
42
) that

\displaystyle a^{2}_{\leq k-1}(x)\leq\exp(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}-{\rm h^{*}}))\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}_{\leq k}(X)\,\big|\,X_{u}=x_{u}\mbox{ and }X_{\leq k-1}=x_{\leq k-1}\big]

By taking Conditional Expectation on both sides,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{U}a^{2}_{\leq k-1})(x)\leq\exp(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}-{\rm h^{*}}))\big(\mathbb{E}_{U}a^{2}_{\leq k}\big)(x).

Finally, we apply this inequality consecutively for
k\in[s]
we obtain

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{U}a)^{2}(x)\leq\exp(-\varepsilon|I^{\prime}|({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}-{\rm h^{*}}))(\mathbb{E}_{U}a^{2})(x).

∎

Corollary 6.6
.

Fix
u\in T\backslash L
and a function
f_{u}(x)
following the form described in Definition
6.4
.
If
I,J\subseteq[d_{u}]
are subsets of
[d_{u}]
of size at least
2
, then
for every
\theta\in[q]
,

(43)

\displaystyle|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I})(\theta)|\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon|I|}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C-{\rm h^{*}}\Big)\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)}

(44)

\displaystyle|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}\cdot f_{u,J})(\theta)|\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon|I\Delta J|}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)}\cdot\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,J})(\theta)},

where
C=C(M,c^{*})
is the constant introduced in Proposition
6.5
,
and
I\Delta J:=(I\setminus J)\cup(J\setminus I)
.

Proof.

For the first statement, it follows from Proposition
6.5
with
a(x)=f_{u,I}(x)
and
I=I^{\prime}
.

To prove the second statement, we begin by noting that the inputs of
f_{u,I}(x)
and
f_{u,J}(x)
do not include

\big(x_{v}\,:\,v\in\bigcup_{i\in J\backslash I}T_{u_{i}}\big)
and

\big(x_{v}\,:\,v\in\bigcup_{i\in I\backslash J}T_{u_{i}}\big)
, respectively.
Thus, we can apply the
Markov Property
and that fact that if
Y,Z,W
are ind pendent then:

\mathbb{E}[g(Y,Z)h(Z,W)]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[g(Y,Z)h(Z,W)|Z]]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[g(Y,Z)|Z]h(Z,W)]

and this in turn becomes:

\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[g(Y,Z)|Z]h(Z,W)|W]=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[g(Y,Z)|Z]\mathbb{E}[h(Z,W)|W]],

to obtain

\displaystyle\big(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}f_{u,J}\big)(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{u,I}(X)\,\Big|\,X_{v}:v\notin\bigcup_{i\in I\backslash J}T_{u_{i}}\Big]\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{u,J}(X)\,\Big|\,X_{v}:v\notin\bigcup_{i\in J\backslash I}T_{u_{i}}\Big]\,\bigg|\,X_{v}=x_{v}:v\not<u\bigg].

In terms of absolute value, by Proposition
6.5
we have

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}f_{u,J})(x)\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{u,I}(X)f_{u,J}(X)\,\Big|\,X_{v}\,:\,v\notin\bigcup_{i\in I\Delta J}T_{u_{i}}\Big]\Big|\,\bigg|\,X_{v}=x_{v}:v\not<u\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{u,I}(X)\,\Big|\,X_{v}\,:\,v\notin\bigcup_{i\in I\setminus J}T_{u_{i}}\Big]\Big|\cdot\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{u,J}(X)\,\Big|\,X_{v}\,:\,v\notin\bigcup_{i\in J\setminus I}T_{u_{i}}\Big]\Big|\,\bigg|\,X_{v}=x_{v}:v\not<u\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sqrt{\exp(-\varepsilon|I\backslash J|({\rm h}(u)-C-{\rm h^{*}}))\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{u,I}^{2}(X)\,\Big|\,X_{v}\,:\,v\notin\bigcup_{i\in I\setminus J}T_{u_{i}}\Big]}

\displaystyle\phantom{AAA}\cdot\sqrt{\exp(-\varepsilon|J\backslash I|({\rm h}(u)-C-{\rm h^{*}}))\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{u,J}^{2}(X)\,\Big|\,X_{v}\,:\,v\notin\bigcup_{i\in J\setminus I}T_{u_{i}}\Big]}\,\bigg|\,X_{v}=x_{v}:v\not<u\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}|I\Delta J|({\rm h}(u)-C-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{u,I}^{2}(X)\,\Big|\,X_{v}\,:\,v\notin\bigcup_{i\in I\setminus J}T_{u_{i}}\Big]\,\bigg|\,X_{v}=x_{v}\,:\,v\not<u\bigg]}

\displaystyle\phantom{AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\Big[f_{u,J}^{2}(X)\,\Big|\,X_{v}\,:\,v\notin\bigcup_{i\in J\setminus I}T_{u_{i}}\Big]\,\bigg|\,X_{v}=x_{v}\,:\,v\not<u\bigg]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}|I\Delta J|({\rm h}(u)-C-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}^{2})(x)\cdot(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,J}^{2})(x)},

where the second to last inequality follows from Hölder’s inequality.

∎

Corollary 6.7
.

There exists
C=C(M,d,c^{*})\geq 1
so that the following holds.
If
u\in T\backslash L
with
{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C(1+\log(R))
, then
for any
f_{u}(x)
in the form as described in Definition
6.4
,

(45)

\displaystyle\forall\theta\in[q],\,\,\frac{1}{2}\cdot\sum_{I\subset[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)\leq(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u})(\theta).

Proof.

Let
C_{0}=C_{0}(M,d,c^{*})
denote the constant introduced in the statement of the Lemma. Its value will be determined along the proof.

The statement of the Corollary is trivial when
d_{u}<2
since in that case
\mathcal{B}_{u}=\emptyset
, implying

f_{u}=0
. From now on, we assume
d_{u}\geq 2
.

First,

(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u})(x)-\sum_{I\in[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(x)=\sum_{\{I,J\}}2(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}\cdot f_{u,J})(x)

where
\sum_{\{I,J\}}
refers to the sum over all unordered pairs
\{I,J\}
with

I
and
J
being distinct subsets of
[d_{u}]
of size at least
2
.

We can apply (
44
) to estimate the absolute value of the difference.

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{\{I,J\}}2(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}\cdot f_{u,J})(x)\Big|

(46)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\{I,J\}}2\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon|I\Delta J|}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}^{2})(x)}\cdot\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,J}^{2})(x)},

where the constant
C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}
is the constant
C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}
introduced in Proposition
6.5
.
By
2|ab|\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
for
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
,

2\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}^{2})(x)}\cdot\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,J}^{2})(x)}\leq(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}^{2})(x)+(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,J}^{2})(x).

Hence,

\displaystyle\eqref{LINeq:h_unorm00}\leq\sum_{I\subset[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}^{2})(x)\Big(\sum_{J\subset[d_{u}]\,:\,I\neq J}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon|I\Delta J|}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\Big).

With
|\{J\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I\Delta J|=i\}|={d_{u}\choose i}\leq d_{u}^{i}
,

(47)

\displaystyle\sum_{J\subset[d_{u}]\,:\,I\neq J}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon|I\Delta J|}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}d_{u}^{i}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon i}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\leq 1/4,

provided that
{\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-\frac{\log(d_{u})}{\varepsilon}-{\rm h^{*}}\geq\frac{16}{\varepsilon}
.

Now, we impose the
first assumption
on
C_{0}
that

C_{0}\geq C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}+\frac{\log(d)}{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\frac{16}{\varepsilon},

then

{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C_{0}(\log(R)+1)\geq C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}+\frac{\log(d_{u})}{\varepsilon}+\frac{16}{\varepsilon}+{\rm h^{*}}.

Hence,

(48)

\displaystyle\Big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u})(x)-\sum_{I\in[d]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(x)\Big|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{I\in[d]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(x)

and the proof follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

Let
C_{0}=C_{0}(M,d,c^{*})
denote the constant introduced in the statement of the Proposition.
Its precise value will be determined along the proof. From Remark
6.3
, it is sufficient to consider the case when
|d_{u}|\geq 2
. Further, it suffices to prove in the case when

(49)

\displaystyle{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C_{0}(\log(R)+1),

since otherwise the statements follow from either
Cauchy-Schwarz or Jenson’s inequality.

Part I: Derivation of (
33
) and (
34
).

First, by (
43
),

(50)

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u})^{2}(\theta))=

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I})(\theta)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon|I|}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\cdot\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]:|I|\geq 2}\exp\Big(-\varepsilon|I|({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\Big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]:|I|\geq 2}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)\Big),

where we applied Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in the last inequality;
the constant
C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}
is the constant
C
introduced in Proposition
6.5
.

With the coarse estimate

\big|\big\{I\subseteq[d_{u}]:|I|=t\big\}\big|={d_{u}\choose i}\leq d_{u}^{t}\leq(Rd)^{t},

we have

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]:|I|\geq 2}\exp\Big(-\varepsilon|I|({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\Big)

(51)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{\infty}\exp\bigg(-\varepsilon t\Big({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-\frac{\log(R)+\log(d)}{\varepsilon}-{\rm h^{*}}\Big)\bigg).

The geometric series above is finite if
{\rm h}(u)
is large enough, and this can be achieved by imposing assumption of
C_{0}
and relying on (
49
).
Now, let us impose the
first assumption
on
C_{0}
:

(52)

\displaystyle C_{0}\geq C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}+(2+2\log(d)+100)/\varepsilon.

Then, by (
49
) we have

{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C_{0}(\log(R)+1)\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}+2\frac{\log(R)+\log(d)}{\varepsilon}+\frac{100}{\varepsilon},

which in term implies the R.H.S. of (
51
) is

\displaystyle\frac{\exp\bigg(-2\varepsilon\Big({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-\frac{\log(R)+\log(d)}{\varepsilon}-{\rm h^{*}}\Big)\bigg)}{1-\exp\bigg(-\varepsilon\Big({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-\frac{\log(R)+\log(d)}{\varepsilon}-{\rm h^{*}}\Big)\bigg)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\exp\bigg(-2\varepsilon\Big({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-\frac{\log(R)+\log(d)}{\varepsilon}-{\rm h^{*}}\Big)\bigg)}{1-e^{-100}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\bigg(-2\varepsilon\Big({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-\frac{\log(R)+\log(d)}{\varepsilon}-{\rm h^{*}}\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\exp\bigg(-2\varepsilon\Big({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-\frac{\log(R)+\log(d)}{\varepsilon}-{\rm h^{*}}-\frac{\log(8)}{2\varepsilon}\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\exp\Big(-2\varepsilon\big({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)

Substituting the above estimate into (
50
), together with (
45
) we have

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u})^{2}(\theta)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\exp\Big(-2\varepsilon\big({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]:|I|\geq 2}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)\Big)

(53)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\exp\Big(-2\varepsilon\big({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u})(\theta).

Therefore, we have derived an inequality which is slightly stronger than (
33
).

To derive (
34
), let us first show
\mathbb{E}f_{u}(X)
is relatively small
using (
53
) and Jesnon’s inequality:

\displaystyle\big(\mathbb{E}[f_{u}(X)]\big)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u})^{2}(X)\big]\leq\frac{1}{2}\exp\Big(-2\varepsilon\big({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\big[f_{u}^{2}(X)\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[f_{u}^{2}(X)\big].

Thus, the variance and the second moment of
f_{u}(X)
are the same up to a factor of
2
:

(54)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{u}^{2}(X)\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[f_{u}^{2}(X)\big]-\big(\mathbb{E}[f_{u}(X)]\big)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[f_{u}^{2}(X)\big].

We conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X)\big]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u})^{2}(X)\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\exp\Big(-2\varepsilon\big({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\mathbb{E}\big[f_{u}^{2}(X)\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp(-2\varepsilon({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}}))\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{u}^{2}(X)\big].

Therefore, we complete the proof of (
34
).

Part II: Derivation of (
35
).

It remains to show (
35
) and the proof is similar.
Fix
I\subset[d_{u}]
with
|I|\geq 2
, let
I^{\prime}=I\backslash\{i\}

and we represent
x\in[q]^{T}
as
(x_{0},x_{1})
, where

\displaystyle x_{0}:=

\displaystyle\big(x_{v}\,:\,v\notin\bigcup_{j\in I^{\prime}}T_{u_{j}}\big)

and

\displaystyle x_{1}:=

\displaystyle\big(x_{v}\,:\,v\in\bigcup_{j\in I^{\prime}}T_{u_{j}}\big).

With this notation, we have
a(x)=a(x_{0})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\tilde{f}_{u,I}(X)a(X)|\big]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{u,I}(X)\,\big|\,X_{0}\big]\cdot a(X_{0})\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{u,I}(X)\,\big|\,X_{0}\big]\big)^{2}\Big]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}(X_{0})\big]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\mathbb{E}\big[f_{u,I}(X)\,\big|\,X_{0}\big]\big)^{2}\Big]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}(X_{0})\big]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}|I\setminus\{i\}|({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[f^{2}_{u,I}(X)\big]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}(X)\big]},

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
6.5
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\tilde{f}_{u}(X)a(X)|\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}|I\setminus\{i\}|({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[f^{2}_{u,I}(X)\big]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}(X)\big]}

(55)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}\exp\big(-\varepsilon|I\setminus\{i\}|({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}})\big)\bigg)^{1/2}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}\mathbb{E}\big[f^{2}_{u,I}(X)\big]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}(X)\big]}.

Next, we impose the
second assumption
on
C_{0}
that

C_{0}\geq C_{\ref{cor: fufuI}},

where
C_{\ref{cor: fufuI}}
is the constant introduced in Corollary
6.7
.
Together our assumption
{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C_{0}(\log(R)+1)
at the beginning of the proof,
we can apply the Corollary and (
54
) to get

(56)

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}\mathbb{E}f^{2}_{u,I}(X)\leq 2\mathbb{E}(f_{u}(X))^{2}\leq 4\mathbb{E}(\tilde{f}_{u}(X))^{2}.

Repeating the same argument as in the proof of (
34
) and relying on the assumption (
52
) of
C_{0}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}\exp(-\varepsilon|I\backslash\{i\}|({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}}))\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\exp\bigg(-\varepsilon t\Big({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop:Bwinwin}}-{\rm h^{*}}-2\frac{\log(Rd)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\bigg)

(57)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\exp\bigg(-\varepsilon\Big({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\Big)\bigg).

Therefore, combining (
56
), (
57
), and (
55
) we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\tilde{f}_{u}(X)a(X)|\big]\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{u}^{2}(X)\big]}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}(X)\big]}.

∎

7.
Induction Step 2: Decay of
f_{k}
and the proof of Theorem
3.6

As a continuation of the inductive step, we adapt the notation introduced in the previous section. Building on the properties of an single component
f_{u}
from Proposition
6.1
, our objective is to deduce variance and covariance decay of
f_{k}
, which is stated in Proposition
7.1
below. Once it is established, we will be ready to prove Theorem
3.6
.

7.1.
Properties of
f_{k}

The main goal of this subsection is to derive the following Proposition.

Proposition 7.1
.

There exists
C=C(M,d,c^{*})\geq 1
so that the following holds.
For any
\rho^{\prime}\in T
satisfying

{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C(1+\log(R)).

Fix a positive integer
k_{1}
such that

\displaystyle{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})\geq k_{1}\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C(1+\log(R)).

Consider a function

\displaystyle f(x)=

\displaystyle c+\sum_{{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{B}_{\leq\rho^{\prime}})}c_{\sigma}\phi_{\sigma}(x)

with
\mathbb{E}f(X)=0
. We decompose
f
according to Lemma
5.1
with the given
k_{1}
.
Then, the following holds:

•

for
k\in[k_{1}+1,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]
,

(58)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]\leq\exp\Big(-\varepsilon\big({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+k-C(\log(R)+1)-2{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X),

•

for
k=k_{1}
,

(59)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k_{1}})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]\leq\exp\Big(-\varepsilon\big({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-k-C(\log(R)+1)\big)\Big)\mathbb{E}f_{k_{1}}^{2}(X),\mbox{ and }

•

for
k_{1}\leq m<k\leq{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})
,

(60)

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[f_{k}(X)f_{m}(X)\big]\big|\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big(k-C(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[f_{k}^{2}(X)\big]\mathbb{E}\big[f_{m}^{2}(X)\big]},

Before we prove the Proposition, let us prove the following second moment bounds for the partial sums of
\tilde{f}_{u}
.

Lemma 7.2
.

There exists a constant
C=C(M,d,c^{*})\geq 1
so that the following holds.
Consider the same description as stated in Proposition
7.1

and
k_{1}\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C(\log(R)+1)
.
Let
(h,k)
be a pair of integers satisfying
k_{1}<h\leq k\leq l^{\prime}
.
For
u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})
, let

\displaystyle f_{h,u}(x)=\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\tilde{f}_{v}(x).

In other words,

f_{h}(x)=\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}f_{h,u}(x).

The following holds:
First, for
u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X)\leq\mathbb{E}f_{h,u}^{2}(X)\leq 2\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X).

Second,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{u\in L_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}f_{h,u}^{2}(X)\leq\mathbb{E}f_{h}^{2}(X)\leq 4\sum_{u\in L_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}f_{h,u}^{2}(X).

Proof.

Let
C_{0}=C_{0}(M,d,\varepsilon^{\prime})
denote the constant introduced in the statement of the Lemma. Its precise value will be determined along the proof.

Let us fix
u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})
. Consider the following conditional expectation of
f_{h,u}(x)
.

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{h}f_{h,u})(x)=\mathbb{E}\big[f_{h,u}(X)\,\big|\,X_{v}=x_{v}\,:\,{\rm h}(v)\geq h\big]=\sum_{v\in D_{h}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})(x_{v}).

Comparing the second moments of
f_{h,u}(x)=\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\tilde{f}_{v}(x)
and
\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})(x_{v})
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\tilde{f}_{v}(X)\Big)^{2}\Big]=

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}^{2}_{v}(X)\big]+\sum_{v,v^{\prime}\in D_{h}(u)\,:\,v\neq v^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{v}(X)\tilde{f}_{v^{\prime}}(X)\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X)\big]+\sum_{(v,v^{\prime})\in(D_{h}(u))^{2}\,:\,v\neq v^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\Big[(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})(X)(\mathbb{E}_{v^{\prime}}\tilde{f}_{v^{\prime}})(X)\,\Big|\,X_{{\mathfrak{p}}(v,v^{\prime})}\Big]\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})(X)\Big)^{2}\Big]+\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X)\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})^{2}(X)\big]\Big)

(61)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\Big(1-\exp\Big(-\varepsilon\big(h-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\Big)\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X)\big],

where the last inequality follow from Proposition
6.1
and

C_{\ref{prop: fu}}
is the constant
C
introduced in Proposition
6.1
.

Here we impose the
first assumption
on
C_{0}
:

C_{0}>10\max\{\varepsilon^{-1},C_{\ref{prop: fu}}\}.

Then, due to
k_{1}\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C_{0}(\log(R)+1)
, we have

\exp(-\varepsilon(h-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}})))\leq\exp(-\varepsilon(k_{1}-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}})))\leq\exp(-\varepsilon\cdot 0.9C_{0})\leq 1/2,

and thus (
61
) can be simplified to

(62)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[f_{h,u}^{2}(X)\big]\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X)\big].

With the lower bound been established, the upper bound can also be derived in the same fashion. Let us first recycle the first three lines of (
61
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\tilde{f}_{v}(X)\Big)^{2}\Big]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})(X)\Big)^{2}+\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{f}_{v}(X))^{2}-\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})^{2}(X)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})(X)\Big)^{2}+\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{f}_{v}(X))^{2}.

Notice that we can apply Lemma
4.2
for the first summand in the above expression.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})(X)\Big)^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})(X_{v})\Big)^{2}\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:deg1T}}R\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})^{2}(X_{v})

where
C_{\ref{lem:deg1T}}
is the constant introduced in Lemma
4.2
.
Again, applying the estimate from Proposition
6.1
we have

C_{\ref{lem:deg1T}}R\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}(\mathbb{E}_{v}\tilde{f}_{v})^{2}(X_{v})\leq C_{\ref{lem:deg1T}}R\exp\Big(-2\varepsilon\big(h-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X).

Here we impose the
second assumption
on
C_{0}
that

(63)

\displaystyle C_{0}\geq C_{\ref{prop: fu}}+\frac{1+\log(C_{\ref{lem:deg1T}})}{2\varepsilon}.

Then, relying on
h>k_{1}\geq{\rm h^{*}}+C_{0}(\log(R)+1)
,

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:deg1T}}R\exp\Big(-2\varepsilon\big(h-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-2\varepsilon\Big(h-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}}-\frac{\log(C_{\ref{lem:deg1T}})+\log(R)}{2\varepsilon}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1,

which in turn implies

\mathbb{E}(f_{h,u}(X))^{2}\leq 2\sum_{v\in D_{h}(u)}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{f}_{v}(X))^{2}.

Now it remains to show the second statement. Notice that
f_{h}=f_{h,\rho^{\prime}}
, we immediately have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{v\in D_{h}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X)\leq\mathbb{E}f_{h}^{2}(X)\leq 2\sum_{v\in D_{h}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X)

Together with

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}f_{k,u}^{2}(X)\leq\sum_{v\in D_{h}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{v}^{2}(X)\leq 2\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}f_{k,u}^{2}(X),

the second statement of the lemma follows.

∎

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Let
C_{0}=C_{0}(M,d,c^{*})
denote the constant introduced in the statement of the Lemma.
Its precise value will be determined along the proof.
Let us make the
first assumption
on
C_{0}
that

C_{0}\geq C_{\ref{lem: fhku}},

where
C_{\ref{lem: fhku}}
is the constant introduced in Lemma
7.2
. Now, we could apply
the statements of the Lemma.

Part 1: Derivation of (
58
).

Fix
k\in[k_{1}+1,l^{\prime}]
. Applying Lemma
7.2
with the parameters
h
and
k
in the Lemma
setting to be
k
,

(64)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(f_{k}(X))^{2}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{f}_{u}(X))^{2}.

The next step is to compare the sum of
\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{u}^{2}(X)
with

\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]
. By Jenson’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{f}_{u})(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})|\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})|\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big].

For each summand, we can apply (
3
) from Lemma
2.2

to get the following estimate.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}(l^{\prime}-k)^{2q}\lambda^{2(l^{\prime}-k)}\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X_{u})\big]

where
C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}
is the constant introduced in the Lemma.
Together with
|D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})|\leq Rd^{l^{\prime}-k}
from the assumption on
T

and
d\lambda^{2}\leq\exp(-1.1\varepsilon)
from the definiton of
\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}(l^{\prime}-k)^{2q}R\exp(-1.1\varepsilon(l^{\prime}-k))\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X_{u})\big]

(65)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\exp\Big(-\varepsilon\Big(l^{\prime}-C^{\prime}(1+\log(R))-k\Big)\Big)\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X_{u})\big]

where we set

C^{\prime}=1+\log\big(1+2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}\max_{n\in\mathbb{N}}n^{2q}\exp(-0.1\varepsilon n)\big)<+\infty.

By Proposition
6.1
we have

(66)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X)\big]\leq\exp(-2\varepsilon(k-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}}))\mathbb{E}(\tilde{f}_{u}(X))^{2},

where
C_{\ref{prop: fu}}
is the constant
C
introduced in the Proposition.
Substituting this inequality into (
65
), together with (
64
)
from first step,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\exp\Big(-\varepsilon\Big(l^{\prime}+k-(C^{\prime}+C_{\ref{prop: fu}})(\log(R)+1)-2{\rm h^{*}}\Big)\Big)\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{f}_{u}(X))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp(-\varepsilon(l^{\prime}+k-(C^{\prime}+C_{\ref{prop: fu}})(\log(R)+1)-2{\rm h^{*}}))\mathbb{E}(f_{k}(X))^{2}.

Now, we impose the
second assumption
on
C_{0}
that

\displaystyle C_{0}\geq(C^{\prime}+C_{\ref{prop: fu}}),

we finished the proof of (
58
).

Part 2: Derivation of (
59
).

Let us consider

h_{k_{1}}(x):=(\mathbb{E}_{k_{1}}f_{k_{1}})(x)=\mathbb{E}\big[f_{k_{1}}(X)\,\big|\,X_{u}=x_{u}\,:\,u\in D_{k_{1}}(\rho^{\prime})\big].

In other words, we may view
h_{k_{1}}(x)
as a linear function with variables
x_{u}
for
u\in D_{k_{1}}(\rho^{\prime})

with
\mathbb{E}h_{k_{1}}(X)=\mathbb{E}f_{k_{1}}(X)=0
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k_{1}})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]\leq\exp

\displaystyle\big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-k_{1}-C_{\ref{prop: inductionBaseSimple}})\big)\mathbb{E}h_{k_{1}}^{2}(X)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-k_{1}-C_{\ref{prop: inductionBaseSimple}})\big)\mathbb{E}f_{k_{1}}^{2}(X)

The first inequality follows from Proposition
3.5
. The second inequality follows from Jensen’s inequality.
Here we impose the
third assumption
on
C_{0}
that

\displaystyle C_{0}\geq C_{\ref{prop: inductionBaseSimple}},

the derivation of (
59
) follows.

Part 3: Derivation of (
60
)

For
w\leq\rho^{\prime}
with
m\leq{\rm h}(w)\leq k
, let

\displaystyle f_{m,w}(x)=\sum_{v\in D_{k}(w)}\tilde{f}_{v}(x).

Let us make a remark that either by second property of
f
from Lemma
5.1
when
m=k_{1}
or by
Lemma
7.2
in the case when
m>k_{1}
, we have the following:
For
w\leq\rho^{\prime}
and
m\leq k^{\prime}\leq{\rm h}(w)
,

(67)

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k^{\prime}}(w)}\mathbb{E}f^{2}_{m,u}(X)\Big)^{1/2}\leq C_{\ref{LINlem:fdecomposition}}R^{2}\big(\mathbb{E}f_{m,w}^{2}(X)\big)^{1/2}.

With this notation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}f_{k}(X)f_{m}(X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{u}(X)f_{m,u}(X)+\sum_{u,u^{\prime}\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})\,:\,u\neq u^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{u}(X)f_{m,u^{\prime}}(X)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{u}(X)f_{m,u}(X)+\sum_{u,u^{\prime}\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})\,:\,u\neq u^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\Big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})(X)(\mathbb{E}_{u^{\prime}}f_{m,u^{\prime}})(X)\,\Big|\,X_{{\mathfrak{p}}(u,u^{\prime})}\Big]\bigg]

(68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})(X)\Big)\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{m,u})(X)\Big)\Big]+\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{u}(X)f_{m,u}(X)

\displaystyle-\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})(X)(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{m,u})(X)\Big].

We will estimate the three summands individually.

Part 3.1: Estimating first summand of (
68
)

First, we apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})(X))(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{m,u})(X))\Big]\bigg|=

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[(\mathbb{E}_{k}f_{k})(X)(\mathbb{E}_{k}f_{m})(X)\Big]\Big|

(69)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{k}f_{k})^{2}(X)\big]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{k}f_{m})^{2}(X)\big]}.

Now, combining (
66
) and (
64
), we have

(70)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{k}f_{k})^{2}(X)\big]}\leq\sqrt{2}\exp\big(-\varepsilon\big(k-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\big)(\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X))^{1/2}.

By setting

C_{1}=C_{\ref{prop: fu}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\log(2)+\log(2C_{\ref{LINlem:fdecomposition}})+2\Big),

we can conclude that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\Big[(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})(X))(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{m,u})(X))\Big]\bigg|

(71)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp(-\varepsilon(k-C_{1}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}))\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)\mathbb{E}f_{m}^{2}(X)},

Part 3.2: Estimating second summand of (
68
)

For the second summand of (
68
), we begin with the estimate for each
u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\tilde{f}_{u}(X)f_{m,u}(X)|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[d_{u}]}\mathbb{E}|\tilde{f}_{u}(X)f_{m,u_{i}}(X)|.

Since for each
i\in[d_{u}]
we have
f_{m,u_{i}}(x)=f_{m,u_{i}}(x_{\leq u_{i}})
, we apply (
35
)
from Proposition
6.1
to
\tilde{f}_{u}
and
a(x)=f_{m,u_{i}}(x)
to get

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[d_{u}]}\mathbb{E}|\tilde{f}_{u}(X)f_{m,u_{i}}(X)|\leq\sum_{i\in[d_{u}]}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big(k-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)(\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}^{2}_{u}(X))^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}f^{2}_{m,u_{i}}(X))^{1/2},

where
C_{\ref{prop: fu}}
is the constant introduced in the Proposition.
Applying Jensen’s inequality and (
67
) with
w=u
and
k^{\prime}=k-1
,

\sum_{i\in[d_{u}]}(\mathbb{E}f^{2}_{m,u_{i}}(X))^{1/2}\leq d_{u}^{1/2}\Big(\sum_{i\in[d_{u}]}\mathbb{E}f^{2}_{m,u_{i}}(X)\Big)^{1/2}\leq(Rd)^{1/2}C_{\ref{LINlem:fdecomposition}}R^{2}\big(\mathbb{E}f_{m,u}^{2}(X)\big)^{1/2}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\tilde{f}_{u}(X)f_{m,u}(X)|\leq

\displaystyle(Rd)^{1/2}C_{\ref{LINlem:fdecomposition}}R^{2}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big(k-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)(\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}^{2}_{u}(X))^{1/2}\big(\mathbb{E}f_{m,u}^{2}(X)\big)^{1/2}

(72)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big(k-C_{2}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)(\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}^{2}_{u}(X))^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}f^{2}_{m,u}(X))^{1/2},

where

C_{2}=\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log(d)+\log(C_{\ref{LINlem:fdecomposition}})\Big)+C_{\ref{prop: fu}}.

Now, returning to the summation, we apply (
72
) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to get

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}_{u}(X)f_{m,u}(X)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}(k-C_{2}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)(\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}^{2}_{u}(X))^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}f^{2}_{m,u}(X))^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}(k-C_{2}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}^{2}_{u}(X)\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}f^{2}_{m,u}(X)\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref{LINlem:fdecomposition}}R^{2}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}(k-C_{2}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\big(\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)\big)^{1/2}\big(\mathbb{E}f_{m}^{2}(X)\big)^{1/2}

(73)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}(k-C_{3}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)\mathbb{E}f_{m}^{2}(X)}.

In the derivation above,
we applied Lemma
7.2
for the term

\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\tilde{f}^{2}_{u}(X)\Big)^{1/2}
and (
67
) for
the term
\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}f^{2}_{m,u}(X)\Big)^{1/2}

in the secont to last inequality. The constant
C_{2}
in the last inequality is defined as

C_{3}=\frac{2}{\varepsilon}(\log(2C_{\ref{LINlem:fdecomposition}})+2)+C_{2}.

Part 3.3: Estimating third summand of (
68
)

It remains to bound the third summand, it can be reduced to the upper bound
for first summand. Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Hölder’s inequality we have

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})(X)(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{m,u})(X)\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})(X)(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{m,u})(X)\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X)\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{m,u})^{2}(X)\Big)^{1/2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbb{E}\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})^{2}(X)\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\mathbb{E}\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{m,u})^{2}(X)\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\exp\big(-\varepsilon\big(k-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\big)\cdot C_{\ref{LINlem:fdecomposition}}R^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)\mathbb{E}f_{m}^{2}(X)}.

where in the last inequality we applied (
70
) and (
67
).

By setting

C_{4}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\log(2)+\log(C_{\ref{LINlem:fdecomposition}})+2\Big),

we conclude that

(74)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{u\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u})(X)(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{m,u})(X)\Big]\Big|\leq

\displaystyle\exp\big(-\varepsilon\big(k-C_{4}(1+\log(R))-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\big)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)\mathbb{E}f_{m}^{2}(X)}.

Now, combining the three estimates of the summands (
71
), (
73
), and (
74
) for
(
68
) we conclude that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}f_{k}(X)f_{m}(X)|\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big(k-C_{5}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)\mathbb{E}f_{m}^{2}(X)},

where

C_{5}:=\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(3)+\max\{C_{1},C_{3},C_{4}\}.

Now we impose the
forth assumption
on
C_{0}
that

C_{0}\geq C_{5},

and (
60
) follows.

∎

7.2.
Proof of Theorem
3.6

Let
C_{0}=C_{0}(M,d,c^{*})
denote the constant introduced in the statement of the Lemma.
Its precise value will be determined along the proof.

Let
k_{1}
be a positive integer with the precise value to be determined later.
Here we impose our
first assumption
on
k_{1}
that

k_{1}\geq C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)+{\rm h^{*}}

where
C_{\ref{prop: fk}}
is a constant that appears in Proposition
7.1
.

Now, let us consider a function
f
described in the Theorem. Without lose of generality,
we may assume
\mathbb{E}f(X)=0
. Then, it is equivalent to estimate the second moments.

Further, let us assume
{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})\geq k_{1}
and the decomposition of
f
according to Lemma
5.1
:

(75)

\displaystyle f(x)=\sum_{k\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}f_{k}(X).

Our first goal is to show

\mathbb{E}f(X)^{2}\simeq\sum_{k\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}f_{k}^{2}(X),

by showing
\mathbb{E}f_{k}(X)f_{m}(X)
is insignificant whenever
k\neq m
.

For
k_{1}\leq m<k
, by Propostion
7.1
,

\displaystyle 2|\mathbb{E}f_{k}(X)f_{m}(X)|\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big(k-C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)(\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X))^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}f_{m}^{2}(X))^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big(k-C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)

\displaystyle+\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big(k-C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\mathbb{E}f_{m}^{2}(X).

Applying the above inequality to bound the second moment of
f(X)
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sum_{k,m\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\mathbb{E}f_{k}(X)f_{m}(X)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)\cdot\Big(1-\sum_{s\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\big(s-C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\big)\Big)\Big).

Notice that there exists
t_{0}
which depends on
\varepsilon
so that

\sum_{t=t_{0}}^{\infty}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}t\Big)\leq\frac{1}{2}.

By setting

k_{1}:=\lceil{\rm h^{*}}+C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)+t_{0}\rceil,

we get

(76)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X).

Our second goal is comparing
\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]

and

\sum_{k\in[k_{1},\infty]}\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)
.
Starting with the variance and
\ell_{\infty}
norm comparison from Lemma
2.2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f)^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{k\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\max_{\theta_{k}\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})(\theta_{k})\big|\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{k\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\max_{\theta_{k}\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})(\theta_{k})\big|\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}\Big(\sum_{k\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})^{2}(X_{\rho})}\Big)^{2},

where
C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}
is the constant introduced in the Lemma.

By (
58
), from Proposition
7.1
, for
k\in[k_{1}+1,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]\leq\exp\big(-\varepsilon(k-{\rm h^{*}})\big)\cdot\exp\Big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)

And for
k=k_{1}=\lceil{\rm h^{*}}+C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)+t_{0}\rceil
, we apply (
59
) to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{k_{1}})^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})\big]\leq

\displaystyle\exp\big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-k_{1}-C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1))\big)\mathbb{E}f_{k_{1}}^{2}(X)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\big(-\varepsilon(-t_{0}-1)\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\Big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-2C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\mathbb{E}f_{k_{1}}^{2}(X).

Substituting these estimate and by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f)^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})]\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}\exp\Big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-2C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\underbrace{\Big(\exp(\varepsilon(t_{0}+1))+\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\exp(-\varepsilon t)\Big)}_{:=C_{1}}\cdot\sum_{k\in[k_{1},{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\mathbb{E}f_{k}^{2}(X)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}C_{1}2\exp\Big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-2C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X).

Now, by taking

\displaystyle C_{0}\geq\max\Big\{2C_{\ref{prop: fk}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\log(C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}C_{1}2)\,,\,C_{\ref{prop: fk}}+t_{0}+1\Big\},

we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f)^{2}(X_{\rho^{\prime}})]\leq\exp\Big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}})\Big)\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X).

It remains to show the case when
{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})\leq k_{1}
. From the assumption that

C_{0}\geq C_{\ref{prop: fk}}+t_{0}+1
and
k_{1}\leq{\rm h^{*}}+C_{\ref{prop: fk}}(\log(R)+1)+t_{0}+1
,
we have

\exp\Big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}\Big)\geq 1.

Hence, the statement follows directly from Jensen’s inequality.

8.
General Case: Base Case

Now, we want to establish Theorem
1.6
, which does not rely on the assumption
c_{M}>0
.
Let us first establish analogues of Assumption
3.3
(the inductive assumption), Proposition
3.5
(the base case), and Theorem
3.1
(the inductive step) in the general case.

Assumption 8.1
.

By stating that
\mathcal{A}
satisfies this assumption with given parameter
{\rm h^{\circ}}
, we mean

{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{A}\subseteq{\bf 2}^{L}\backslash\emptyset
is closed under decomposition, and the following holds:

For every
u\in T
and any
{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq u}
-polynomials functions
f
and
g
, we have

(77)

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{u}f)(X)\big]\leq\exp(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})){\rm Var}\big[f(X)\big].

Further, suppose
\mathbb{E}f=\mathbb{E}g=0
and
{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}
.
Notice that by the
Markov Property
,
(\mathbb{E}_{u}fg)(x)
,
(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2})(x)
, and
(\mathbb{E}_{u}g^{2})(x)

are functions of
x_{u}
.
Then,

(78)

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{u}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg|\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g^{2})(\theta)}.

The main difference of this assumption and Assumption
3.3
is the difference of
(
78
) and (
12
).

Proposition 8.2
.

Consider the rooted tree
T
and transition matrix
M
described in Theorem
1.6
.
There exists
C=C(M,d)\geq 1
such that
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
satisfies Assumption
8.1

with some parameter
{\rm h^{\circ}}
satisfying

(79)

\displaystyle{\rm h^{\circ}}\leq C(\log(R)+1).

Theorem 8.3
.

Consider the rooted tree
T
and transition matrix
M
described in Theorem
1.6
.
There exists
C=C(M,d)>1
so that the following holds.
Suppose
\mathcal{A}
satisfies Assumption
8.1
with some parameter
{\rm h^{\circ}}
.
Let
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A})
(see Definition
1.10
). Then,
\mathcal{B}
satisfies
Assumption
8.1
with parameter

{\rm h^{\circ}}+C(\log(R)+1).

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

The proof of Theorem
1.6
is analogous to that of Theorem
3.1
, employing a similar strategy by leveraging Proposition
8.2
and Theorem
8.3
in the former, and Proposition
3.5
and Theorem
3.6
in the latter.

∎

In this section we will prove the Base Case Proposition
8.2
.

Lemma 8.4
.

There exists a constant
C=C(M,\varepsilon)\geq 1
so such that for any
\rho^{\prime}\in T
and

0\leq m\leq{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})
:

Consider two degree 1 polynomials
f
and
g
with variables
(x_{u}\,:\,u\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime}))
.
Suppose

f(X)=\sum_{u\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}f_{u}(X)\mbox{ almost surely,}

where
f_{u}(x)=f_{u}(x_{u})
and
\mathbb{E}[f_{u}(X)]=0
, and we assume the same conditions for the polynomial
g
and
g_{u}
. Then,

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg\big|\leq CR\exp(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m))\sqrt{\sum_{u\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)}\sqrt{\sum_{u\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}g_{u}^{2}(X)}.

Proof.

Let
C_{0}=C_{0}(M,d)
denote the constant introduced in the statement of the Lemma.
Its value will be determined along the proof.

First of all,

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg\big|=

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}\Big|\sum_{u,v\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}\left((\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\Big|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\big|.

Our proof will be carried out by estimating each summand.
Fix any pair
u,v\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})
and consider

\|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{u}g_{v})-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\|_{\infty}=\max_{\theta}\big|\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}\big[f_{u}(X)g_{v}(X)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\,\big|\,X_{\rho^{\prime}}=\theta\big]\big|.

Let
w=\rho(u,v)
.
Since
f_{u}
and
g_{u}
are functions of
x_{\leq w}
, relying on the
Markov Property
we know
the function
(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}\cdot g_{v})(x)
is a function of
x_{w}
with expected value
\mathbb{E}f_{u}(X)g_{v}(X)
.
With

(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{u}g_{v})(x_{\rho^{\prime}})=\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}g_{v})(X_{w})\,\big|\,X_{\rho^{\prime}}=x_{\rho^{\prime}}],

applying (
5
) from Lemma
2.2
,

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{u}g_{v})-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-{\rm h}(w))^{q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-{\rm h}(w)}\big\|(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\big\|_{\infty},

where
C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}
is the
M
-dependent constant introduced in the Lemma.

Next, we will estimate
\big\|(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\big\|_{\infty}
.
In the case
u\neq v
, there exists
i\neq j
such that
u\leq w_{i}
and
v\leq w_{j}
, which in turn implies that

(X_{\leq u}\,|\,X_{w}=x_{w})
and
(X_{\leq v}\,|\,X_{w}=x_{w})
are jointly independent by the
Markov Property
. Thus,

(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)=(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u})(\theta)(\mathbb{E}_{w}g_{v})(\theta),

which implies

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)|\leq

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u})(\theta)|\cdot\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{w}g_{v})(\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{2}({\rm h}(w)-m)^{2q}\lambda^{2({\rm h}(w)-m)}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)\mathbb{E}g_{v}^{2}(X)},

where we applied (
5
) and (
4
) from
Lemma
2.2
in the last inequality.
If
u=v
, then the same estimate follows immediately without relying on (
5
).

Now, we convert the above estimate to that of
\|(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\|_{\infty}
,
which relies on the simple bound that
|\mathbb{E}f_{u}(X)g_{v}(X)|\leq\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)|
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\big\|(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\big\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{w}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{2}({\rm h}(w)-m)^{2q}\lambda^{2({\rm h}(w)-m)}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)\mathbb{E}g_{v}^{2}(X)}.

Together we conclude that for a pair
u,v\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})
with
w=\rho(u,v)
,

\displaystyle\|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{u}g_{v})-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}({\rm h}(w)-m)^{2q}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-{\rm h}(w))^{q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+{\rm h}(w)-2m}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)g_{v}^{2}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+{\rm h}(w)-2m}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)g_{v}^{2}(X)}.

Relying on this estimate,
we are ready to bound the
l_{\infty}
-norm of
(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(x_{\rho^{\prime}})-\mathbb{E}fg
.

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg\big|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[m,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\sum_{w\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\sum_{u,v\,:\,\rho(u,v)=w}\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho}f_{u}g_{v})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}g_{v}\big|

(80)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[m,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\sum_{w\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\sum_{u,v\,:\,\rho(u,v)=w}2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+k-2m}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)g_{v}^{2}(X)}.

Next, relaxing the condition
\rho(u,v)=w
in the summation,

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: DotDeq100}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[m,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\sum_{w\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}\sum_{u,v\in D_{m}(w)}2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+k-2m}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)g_{v}^{2}(X)}

(81)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[m,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\sum_{w\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+k-2m}\big(\sum_{u\in D_{m}(w)}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)}\big)\big(\sum_{u\in D_{m}(w)}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}g_{u}^{2}(X)}\big).

Notice the inequality
\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{|t_{i}|}{n}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i\in[n]}\frac{|t_{i}|^{2}}{n}}
follows from Jenson’s inequality applying to the function
t\mapsto t^{2}
and the uniform measure on
[n]
.
Now apply this inequality to the collection
\{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)}\}
and
\{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}g_{u}^{2}(X)}\}
respectively, together with

|D_{m}(w)|\leq Rd^{{\rm h}(w)-m},
from our tree asscumption,
we have

\displaystyle\eqref{eq: DotDeg101}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[m,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}\sum_{w\leq\rho^{\prime}\,:\,w\in D_{k}(\rho^{\prime})}2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+k-2m}Rd^{k-m}\sqrt{\sum_{u\in D_{m}(w)}\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)}\sqrt{\sum_{u\in D_{m}(w)}\mathbb{E}g_{u}^{2}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[m,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+k-2m}Rd^{k-m}

\displaystyle\phantom{AAAAA}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{w\leq\rho^{\prime}\,:\,{\rm h}(w)=k}\sum_{u\in D_{m}(w)}\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{w\leq\rho^{\prime}\,:\,{\rm h}(w)=k}\sum_{u\in D_{m}(w)}\mathbb{E}g_{u}^{2}(X)}

(82)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[m,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+k-2m}Rd^{k-m}\sqrt{\sum_{u\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)}\sqrt{\sum_{u\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}g_{u}^{2}(X)},

where the last inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Finally,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[m,{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})]}2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\lambda^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})+k-2m}Rd^{k-m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}R({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\cdot({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)\lambda^{h(\rho^{\prime})-m}\max_{k\in[m,h(\rho^{\prime})]}\lambda^{k-m}d^{k-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}R({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q}\cdot({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)\big(\max\{d\lambda^{2},\,\lambda\}\big)^{{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}R({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)^{3q+1}\exp(-1.1\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}R\exp(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)),

where

C_{0}=2C_{\ref{lem:Mbasis}}^{3}\max_{n\in\mathbb{N}}n^{3q+1}\exp(-0.1\varepsilon n)<+\infty

is a constant depending on
M
and
\varepsilon
. Combining the above estimate with (
82
) we conclude that

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg\big|\leq C_{0}R\exp\big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m)\big)\sqrt{\sum_{u\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}f_{u}^{2}(X)}\sqrt{\sum_{u\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime})}\mathbb{E}g_{u}^{2}(X)},

and the lemma follows.
∎

The statement of Lemma
8.4
together with Proposition
4.3
implies the following:

Corollary 8.5
.

There exists a constant
C=C(M,\varepsilon)\geq 1
so that the following holds.
For
\rho^{\prime}\in T
and
0\leq m\leq{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})
,
consider two degree 1 polynomials
f
and
g
with variables
(x_{u}\,:\,u\in D_{m}(\rho^{\prime}))

with
\mathbb{E}f(X)=\mathbb{E}g(X)=0
.
Notice that by the
Markov Property
,
(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(x)
is a function of
x_{\rho^{\prime}}
.
Then,

\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg\big|\leq CR^{4}\exp(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-m))\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}g^{2}(X)}.

Remark 8.6
.

By taking the degree 1 polynomial
f=g
with the assumption that
\mathbb{E}f(X)=0
,
we get

(83)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(X)-\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)\big]^{2}\leq\Big(\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg\big|\Big)^{2}\leq C^{2}R^{6}\exp(-2\varepsilon{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime}))(\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X))^{2}.

In other words, if
{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})
is sufficiently large,

(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(X_{\rho^{\prime}})
is almost the same as
\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)
with a small fluctuation.
Let us state this as a seperate lemma.

Lemma 8.7
.

There exists
C=C(M,d)
so that the following holds. For
\rho^{\prime}\in T

with

{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})\geq\frac{C(\log(R)+1)}{\varepsilon},

any degree 1 polynomial
f
of variables
(x_{u}\,:\,u\in L_{\rho^{\prime}})
with
\mathbb{E}f(X)=0
satisfies

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(\theta)\leq 2\min_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(\theta).

Proof.

By Corollary
8.5
, for every
\theta\in[q]
,

\big|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)\big|\leq C_{\ref{cor: DotDeg1}}R^{4}\exp(-\varepsilon{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime}))\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X).

where
C_{\ref{cor: DotDeg1}}
is the constant introduced in Lemma
8.5
.
Now, we set the constant described in the lemma as

C=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\log(4C_{\ref{cor: DotDeg1}})+4\Big),

which implies

C_{\ref{lem:DotDeg1}}R\exp(-\varepsilon{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime}))\leq\frac{1}{4}\exp\big(-\varepsilon({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-C(\log(R)+1))\big).

Then, with
{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})\geq C(\log(R)+1)

\displaystyle|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)|\leq\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X),

which in term implies

\frac{\max_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(\theta)}{\min_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(\theta)}\leq\frac{\frac{5}{4}\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)}{\frac{3}{4}\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)}<2.

∎

Proof of Proposition
8.2
.

Let
C_{0}
denote the constant introduced in the statement of the Proposition.
Its precise value will be determined along the proof.

Let
\rho^{\prime}\in T
with
{\rm h}^{\prime}:={\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})
.
By Lemma
4.9
, any degree-1 polynomial
f(x)
with variables

(x_{u}\,:u\in L_{\rho^{\prime}})
satisfies

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f)(X)\big]\leq C_{\ref{lem:deg1}}R^{4}({\rm h}^{\prime})^{2q}(d\lambda^{2})^{{\rm h}^{\prime}}{\rm Var}[f(X)],

where
C_{\ref{lem:deg1}}
denotes the
M
-dependent constant introduced in the Lemma.
For the term in front of
{\rm Var}[f(X)]
,

\displaystyle C_{\ref{lem:deg1}}R^{4}({\rm h}^{\prime})^{2q}(d\lambda^{2})^{{\rm h}^{\prime}}\leq C_{\ref{lem:deg1}}R^{4}({\rm h}^{\prime})^{2q}\exp(-1.1\varepsilon{\rm h}^{\prime})\leq\exp\big(-\varepsilon\big({\rm h}^{\prime}-C_{1}(\log(R)+1)\big)\big),

where

C_{1}:=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\log(C_{\ref{lem:deg1}})+4+\max_{n\in\mathbb{N}}n^{2q}\exp(-0.1\varepsilon q)\Big).

Thus, if we impose the
first assumption
on
C_{0}
that

C_{0}\geq C_{1},

then the first condition (
77
) in Assumption
8.1

holds for
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
if we take
{\rm h^{\circ}}\geq C_{0}(1+\log(R))
.

It remains to establish (
78
).
Let
f,g
be two degree-1 polynomials in the variables
(x_{u}\,:u\in L_{\rho^{\prime}})

satisfying
\mathbb{E}f(X)=\mathbb{E}g(X)=0
.
First, by Corollary
8.5
,

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg|\leq C_{\ref{cor: DotDeg1}}R^{4}\exp(-\varepsilon{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime}))\sqrt{\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}g^{2}(X)},

where
C_{\ref{cor: DotDeg1}}
is the constant introduced in Corollary
8.5
.
Next, we would like to apply Lemma
8.7
.
Assuming

{\rm h}(u)\geq\frac{C_{\ref{lem: deg1f^2}}(\log(R)+1)}{\varepsilon}

where
C_{\ref{lem: deg1f^2}}
is the constant introduced in the Lemma, we can apply the lemma to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)\leq 2\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(\theta)

and the same holds for
g
.
Together we may conclude that

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg|\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{\ref{cor: DotDeg1}}R^{4}\exp(-\varepsilon{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime}))\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}f^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{\rho^{\prime}}g^{2})(\theta)}

Now, we impose the
second assumption
on
C_{0}
that

C_{0}\geq\max\Big\{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\log(2C_{\ref{cor: DotDeg1}})+4\Big),\,\frac{C_{\ref{lem: deg1f^2}}}{\varepsilon}\Big\}.

Then, we conclude that

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{u}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg|\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\varepsilon\big({\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)\big)\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g^{2})(\theta)}

provided that

{\rm h}(\rho^{\prime})\geq C_{0}(\log(R)+1).

Therefore, we can conclude that
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
satisfies Assumption
8.1
with

{\rm h^{\circ}}=C_{0}(\log(R)+1).

∎

9.
Inductive Step in General Case

The goal in this section is to prove Theorem
8.3
. Let us restate the theorem here:

Theorem
.

Consider the rooted tree
T
and transition matrix
M
described in Theorem
1.6
.
There exists
C=C(M,d)>1
so that the following holds.
Suppose
\mathcal{A}
satisfies Assumption
8.1
with some parameter
{\rm h^{\circ}}
.
Let
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A})
(see Definition
1.10
). Then,
\mathcal{B}
satisfies
Assumption
8.1
with parameter

{\rm h^{\circ}}+C(\log(R)+1).

In this section, we fix a subcollection
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfying Assumption
8.1

with a given parameter
{\rm h^{\circ}}
and let
\mathcal{B}={\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{A}})
.

We begin with the following lemma, which allows us to recycle some of the results from
the case
c_{M}>0
.

Lemma 9.1
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies Assumption
8.1
with parameter
{\rm h^{\circ}}
. Then,
then
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies Assumption
3.3
with

{\rm h^{*}}={\rm h^{\circ}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2)
and
c^{*}=\frac{1}{2}
.

Proof.

Let
f
be a
{\mathcal{A}}_{\leq v}
-polynomial.
If we set
{\rm h^{*}}\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}
, then (
11
) follows immediately from (
77
).

Now, we assume that
\mathbb{E}f(X)=0
and
{\rm h}(v)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}
. We could apply (
78
) with
g=f
to get

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{v}f^{2})(\theta)-\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X)\big|\leq\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(v)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\mathbb{E}f^{2}(X).

If
\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(v)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\leq\frac{1}{2}
, or equivalently,

{\rm h}(v)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2),

then, for every
\theta\in[q]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}h^{2}(X)\leq(\mathbb{E}_{v}h^{2})(\theta)\leq\frac{3}{2}\mathbb{E}h^{2}(X).

Therefore, if we set
{\rm h^{*}}\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2)
and
c^{*}=\frac{1}{2}
,
both (
11
) and (
12
) hold.
∎

In the remainning of this section, we set

(84)

\displaystyle{\rm h^{*}}={\rm h^{\circ}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2)\mbox{ and }c^{*}=\frac{1}{2},

and we will rely on the fact that
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies Assumption
3.3
with these two parameters.
In particular, we could apply Theorem
3.6
to show
the existence of
C_{\ref{LINthm:inductionSimple}}=C(M,\varepsilon,1/2)

such that for any
{\mathcal{B}}_{\leq v}
-polynomial
f
,

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\big[(\mathbb{E}_{v}f)(X)\big]\leq\exp\Big(-\varepsilon\big({\rm h}(v)-{\rm h^{\circ}}+C_{\ref{LINthm:inductionSimple}}(\log(R)+1)\big)\Big){\rm Var}\big[f(X)\big].

Therefore, to establish Theorem
8.3
, it remains to show the existence of
C=C(M,d)

so that any
{\mathcal{B}}_{\leq v}
-polynomials
f
and
g
with
{\rm h}(v)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}+C(\log(R)+1)

and
\mathbb{E}f(X)=\mathbb{E}g(X)=0
satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{v}fg)(\theta)-\mathbb{E}fg|\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(v)-{\rm h^{\circ}}-C(\log(R)+1))\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{v}f^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}_{v}g^{2})(\theta)}.

To establish the above inequality, the higher level structure is essentially the same as that for deriving
Theorem
3.6
.
We again decompose
f
and
g
according to Lemma
5.1
.
To the proof of the theorem, similarly it contains three steps:

(1)

Establish properties of
\tilde{f}_{u}
and
\tilde{g}_{u}
, see Proposition
9.2
.

(2)

Estalbish properties of
f_{k}
and
g_{k}
, see Proposition
9.6
.

(3)

Establish Theorem
8.3
.

9.1.
Properties of
f_{u}

The main goal we want to prove in this subsection is the following Proposition.

Proposition 9.2
.

There exsits
C=C(M,d)\geq 1
so that the following holds.
For a given
u\in T\backslash L
with

{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}+C(\log(R)+1),

suppose
f_{u}
and
g_{u}
are two functions which are linear combination of

\psi_{\sigma}(x)
with
\sigma\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{B}}_{u})
. Then,
for any
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in[q]
,

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g_{u}^{2})(\theta)}.

With a minor modification to our approach, we are able to obtain an analogous result wherein
f_{u}
and
g_{u}
are substituted by
\tilde{f}_{u}
and
\tilde{g}_{u}
, respectively:

Corollary 9.3
.

There exsits
C=C(M,d)\geq 1
so that the following holds.
For a given
u\in T\backslash L
with

{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}+C(\log(R)+1),

suppose
f_{u}
and
g_{u}
are two functions which are linear combination of

\psi_{\bf S}(x)
with
{\bf S}\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{B}}_{u})
. Then,
for any
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in[q]
,

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u}\tilde{g}_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u}\tilde{g}_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u}^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{g}_{u}^{2})(\theta)}.

Let us prove Corollary first.

Proof.

Let
C_{0}
denote the constant introduced in the Corollary. Its value will be dervied during the proof.

From the identity

\tilde{f}_{u}(x)\tilde{g}_{u}(x)=f_{u}(x)g_{u}(x)-f_{u}(x)\mathbb{E}g_{u}(X)-\mathbb{E}f_{u}(X)g_{u}(x)+\mathbb{E}f_{u}(X)\mathbb{E}g_{u}(X),

it follows that

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u}\tilde{g}_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u}\tilde{g}_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|\leq

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|+|\mathbb{E}g_{u}(X)|\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle+|\mathbb{E}f_{u}(X)|\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}g_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}g_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|+4\max_{\theta^{\prime}}|\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}(\theta)|\max_{\theta^{\prime}}|\mathbb{E}_{u}g_{u}(\theta)|.

First, we apply Propostion
6.1
with the fact that
{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies Assumption
8.1
with parameter
{\rm h^{*}}={\rm h^{\circ}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2)
and
c^{*}=\frac{1}{2}
,

\max_{\theta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u})^{2}(\theta)\leq\exp(-2\varepsilon({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}))\max_{\theta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u})(\theta^{\prime})

where
C_{\ref{prop: fu}}=C(M,d,\frac{1}{2})
is the constant introduced in the Proposition.

Second, applying Proposition
9.2
with
f_{u}=g_{u}
we have

\displaystyle\big|\max_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}^{2})(\theta)-\min_{\theta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}^{2})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop: fugu}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}^{2})(\theta)

where
C_{\ref{prop: fugu}}
is the constant introduced in Proposition
9.2
.

Let us impose the
first assumption
that
C_{0}\geq C_{\ref{prop: fugu}}
.
Then, with
{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}+C_{0}(\log(R)+1)
, we can conclude that

\max_{\theta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\leq 2\min_{\theta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u})(\theta^{\prime}).

Clearly, the same derivation also holds for
g_{u}
.

Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u}\tilde{g}_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}\tilde{f}_{u}\tilde{g}_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle+8\exp(-2\varepsilon({\rm h}(u)-C_{1}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{*}}))\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g_{u}^{2})(\theta)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop: fugu}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g_{u}^{2})(\theta)}

\displaystyle+8\exp\Big(-2\varepsilon\Big({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{prop: fu}}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{\circ}}-\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2)\Big)\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g_{u}^{2})(\theta)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{0}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g_{u}^{2})(\theta)},

where the last inequality follows by imposing the
second assumption
on
C_{0}
that

C_{0}\geq\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2)+\max\Big\{C_{\ref{prop: fugu}},C_{\ref{prop: fu}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2)+\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\log(8)\Big\}.

This completes the proof of the Corollary.
∎

The main technical part for proving Proposition
9.2
is the following:

Lemma 9.4
.

For any
u\in T
with
\exp\big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\big)\leq\frac{1}{4Rd}
, the following holds:
Let
I\subset[d_{u}]
be a subset of size at least 2.
For any
a(x)
and
b(x)
which are linear combinations of
\psi_{\sigma}(x)
with
\sigma\in{\mathcal{B}}_{u}
satisfying
I(\sigma)=I
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in[q]}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}ab)(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}ab)(\theta^{\prime})\big|\leq

\displaystyle 4dR\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}a^{2})(\theta)\cdot\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}b^{2})(\theta)}.

Remark 9.5
.

From the assumption that
{\rm h}(u)
satisfies

\displaystyle 4dR\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\leq 1\Leftrightarrow{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(4dR).

By taking
a(x)=b(x)
we have

(85)

\displaystyle\max_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}a^{2})(\theta)\leq 2\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}a^{2})(\theta).

Proof.

Let
u
,
a(x)
, and
b(x)
be the vertex and functions described in the Lemma.
Let us introduce some notations for the ease of expressing the calculation later.
For brevity, let

\delta=\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big).

For
x\in[q]^{T}
, let

x_{u,I}=(x_{u_{i}})_{i\in I}.

For any given function
h(x)
with variables in
(x_{v}\,:\,v\in\bigcup_{i\in I}T_{u_{i}})
, we define

(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}h)(x):=\mathbb{E}\Big[h(X)\,\Big|\,\forall v\notin\bigcup_{i\in I}\{w<u_{i}\},\,X_{v}=x_{v}\Big].

Observe that

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a)(x),\,(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}b)(x)\mbox{, and }(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}ab)(x)

are functions with input
x_{u,I}
. This is due to the fact that
a
and
b
–and consequently
ab
– are functions of variables
(x_{v}\,:\,x_{v}\in\bigcup_{i\in I}L_{u_{i}})
and
Markov Property
.

Claim:
The function
x_{u,I}\mapsto(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}ab)(x_{u,I})
is Lipschitz continuous with respect to the Hamming Distance with Lipschitz constant

(86)

\displaystyle 2\delta\sqrt{\max_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a^{2})(x_{u,I})}\sqrt{\max_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}b^{2})(x_{u,I})}.

We begin with the proof of the claim.
Fix an index
i_{0}\in I
. Without lose of generality, we assume
I=[k]
and
i_{0}=1
. For
x\in[q]^{T}
, let

x_{i}=x_{\leq u_{i}}

for
i\leq[d_{u}]
, and set

x_{0}=(x_{2},\dots,x_{k}).

With this notation above, we can express

\displaystyle a(x)=

\displaystyle a(x_{0},x_{1})

and

\displaystyle b(x)=

\displaystyle b(x_{0},x_{1}).

Fix any value of
x_{0}
, the function

\displaystyle x_{1}\mapsto a(x_{0},x_{1})

is a linear combination of
\tilde{\phi}_{\sigma_{1}}(x_{1})
with
\sigma_{1}\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{A}}_{\leq u})
. Notably, this implies that

\mathbb{E}a(x_{0},X_{1})=0
.
The same properties hold for the function
x_{1}\mapsto b(x_{0},x_{1})
.

Now, given the assumption
\exp\big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\big)\leq\frac{1}{4Rd}
implies
{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}
,
we can apply (
78
) from Assumption
8.1
to get that

\displaystyle\max_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in[q]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[a(x_{0},X_{1})b(x_{0},X_{1})\,\big|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta_{1}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[a(x_{0},X_{1})b(x_{0},X_{1})\,\big|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta_{2}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{\theta\in[q]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[a(x_{0},X_{1})b(x_{0},X_{1})\,\big|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta_{1}\big]-\mathbb{E}a(x_{0},X_{1})b(x_{0},X_{1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta\sqrt{\min_{\theta}\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}(x_{0},X_{1})\,\big|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta\big]\min_{\theta}\mathbb{E}\big[b^{2}(x_{0},X_{1})\,\big|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta\big]}.

For any
x\in[q]^{T}
, let
x_{u_{0}}=(x_{u_{2}},x_{u_{3}},\dots,x_{u_{d_{u}}})
. By the
Markov Property
, for any
\theta\in[q]
,

\displaystyle(X_{0}\,|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}},X_{u_{1}}=\theta)=

\displaystyle(X_{0}\,|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}})\mbox{ and}

\displaystyle(X_{1}\,|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}},X_{u_{1}}=\theta)=

\displaystyle(X_{u_{1}}\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta)

are jointly independent. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[a(X_{0},X_{1})b(X_{0},X_{1})\,\big|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}},X_{u_{1}}=\theta\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[a(Y_{0},X_{1})b(Y_{0},X_{1})\,\big|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta\big]

where
Y_{0}
is an independent copy of
(X_{0}\,|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}})
. We have

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[a(X_{0},X_{1})b(X_{0},X_{1})\,\big|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}},X_{u_{1}}=\theta\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[a(X_{0},X_{1})b(X_{0},X_{1})\,\big|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}},X_{u_{1}}=\theta^{\prime}\big]\big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[a(Y_{0},X_{1})b(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[a(Y_{0},X_{1})b(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta^{\prime}]\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}}\Big[\big|\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[a(Y_{0},X_{1})b(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[a(Y_{0},X_{1})b(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta^{\prime}]\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}}\Big[\big(\min_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[a^{2}(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta]\big)^{1/2}\cdot\big(\min_{\theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[b^{2}(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta^{\prime}]\big)^{1/2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta\big(\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}}\big[\min_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[a^{2}(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta]\big]\big)^{1/2}\cdot\big(\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}}\big[\min_{\theta^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[b^{2}(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta^{\prime}]\big]\big)^{1/2},

where the last inequality follows from Hölder’s inequality. Further,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}}\big[\min_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[a^{2}(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta]\big]\leq

\displaystyle\min_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}}\big[\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[a^{2}(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta]\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\theta}\mathbb{E}\big[a^{2}(X)\,\big|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}},X_{u_{1}}=\theta\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a^{2})(x_{u,I}).

Applying the same derivation to
b
we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}}\big[\min_{\theta}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[b^{2}(Y_{0},X_{1})\,|\,X_{u_{1}}=\theta]\big]\leq\max_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}b^{2})(x_{u,I}).

Therefore, our claim (
86
) follows: For any
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in[q]
,

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}\big[a(X)b(X)\,\big|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}},x_{u_{1}}=\theta\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[a(X)b(X)\,\big|\,X_{u_{0}}=x_{u_{0}},x_{u_{1}}=\theta^{\prime}\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta\sqrt{\max_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}a^{2})(x_{u,I})\max_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}b^{2})(x_{u,I})}.

With the Lipschitz continuity been established, essentially the lemma follows when
\delta
is sufficiently small. Let us proceed with the remaining argument. Let

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{u,I}=

\displaystyle{\rm argmin}_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a^{2})(x_{u,I})

and

\displaystyle x^{\prime\prime}_{u,I}=

\displaystyle{\rm argmax}_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a^{2})(x_{u,I}).

Applying (
86
) with the assumption
a(x)=b(x)
and the fact
|I|\leq d_{u}
,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a^{2})(x^{\prime\prime}_{u,I})-(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a^{2})(x^{\prime}_{u,I})\leq

\displaystyle 2d_{u}\delta(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a^{2})(a^{\prime\prime}_{u,I}),

and hence

(87)

\displaystyle\max_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a^{2})(x_{u,I})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-2d_{u}\delta}\min_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u,I}a^{2})(x_{u,I})\leq\frac{1}{1-2d_{u}\delta}\min_{s}(\mathbb{E}_{u}a^{2})(s),

provided that
2d_{u}\delta<1
.

Again, the same derivation also holds for
b
. Combining (
86
) and (
87
) we conclude that for any
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in[q]
,

\displaystyle|(\mathbb{E}_{u}ab)(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}ab)(\theta^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle|\max_{x_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}ab)(x_{u,I})-\min_{x^{\prime}_{u,I}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}ab)(x^{\prime}_{u,I})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2d_{u}\delta}{1-2d_{u}\delta}\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}a^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}b^{2})(\theta^{\prime})}.

With our assumption on the tree
T
that
d_{u}\leq Rd
, our assumption

\delta=\exp\left(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\right)\leq\frac{1}{4Rd},

implies that

\frac{2d_{u}\delta}{1-2d_{u}\delta}\leq 4Rd\delta.

We conclude that

\displaystyle|(\mathbb{E}_{u}ab)(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}ab)(\theta^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle 4Rd\exp\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\sqrt{\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}a^{2})(\theta)\min_{\theta^{\prime}}(\mathbb{E}_{u}b^{2})(\theta^{\prime})}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
9.2
.

Let
C_{0}=C_{0}(M,d)
denote the constant introduced in the statement of the Proposition.
Recall the decomposition of
f_{u}
into
f_{u,I}
from Definition
6.4
, consider the decomposition

f_{u}(x)=\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}f_{u,I}(x)\mbox{ and }g_{u}(x)=\sum_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}g_{u,I}(x).

The proof of the Proposition will proceed by bounding summands in the formula below:

(88)

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|\leq\sum_{I,J\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|,|J|\geq 2}\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,J})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,J})(\theta^{\prime})\big|.

Estimate of summands in (
88
):

For any
I,J\subseteq[d_{u}]
with
|I|,|J|\geq 2
, we have two cases to consider:
First, we consider the case
I\neq J
.
Notice that, by Lemma
9.1
,

{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies Assumption
3.3
with parameters

({\rm h^{\circ}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2),\,\frac{1}{2})
.
This allows us to invoke Corollary
6.6
, yielding

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,J})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,J})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{\theta\in[q]}|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,J})(\theta)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon|I\Delta J|}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{cor: fuI}}-{\rm h^{\circ}}-\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2))\Big)\big(\max_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)\big)^{1/2}\cdot\big(\max_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g^{2}_{u,J})(\theta)\big)^{1/2},

where
C_{\ref{cor: fuI}}=C_{\ref{cor: fuI}}(M,d,\frac{1}{2})
is the constant introduced in the Corollary.

Let us impose the
first assumption
on
C_{0}
that

C_{0}\geq\frac{2}{\varepsilon}(1+\log(4d)),

which implies that
{\rm h}(u)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}+C_{0}(\log(R)+1)\geq{\rm h^{\circ}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(4dR)
. With this assumption, we could apply the remark (
85
) of Lemma
9.4
to get

\big(\max_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)\big)^{1/2}\leq 2\big(\min_{\theta}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)\big)^{1/2}

and the same holds for
g_{u,J}
. Hence, for
I\neq J
we have

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,J})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,J})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon|I\Delta J|}{2}({\rm h}(u)-C_{\ref{cor: fuI}}-{\rm h^{\circ}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2))\Big)\big(\min_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)\big)^{1/2}\cdot\big(\min_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g^{2}_{u,J})(\theta)\big)^{1/2}.

Second, we consider the case
I=J
. Here we simply apply Lemma
9.4
, yielding

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,I})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,I})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4Rd\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}({\rm h}(u)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)\big(\min_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)\big)^{1/2}\cdot\big(\min_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g^{2}_{u,J})(\theta)\big)^{1/2}.

Let us unify the above two estimates by introducing

C_{1}=\max\Big\{C_{\ref{cor: fuI}}+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(2)+\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\log(8),\,\frac{2}{\varepsilon}(1+\log(24d))\Big\}.

Then,

(89)

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,J})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u,I}g_{u,J})(\theta^{\prime})\big|

(90)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{1}{6}\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\max\{|I\Delta J|,1\}({\rm h}(u)-C_{1}(\log(R)+1)-{\rm h^{\circ}})\Big)}_{:=a_{I,J}}\underbrace{\big(\min_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{u}f^{2}_{u,I})(\theta)\big)^{1/2}}_{:=\alpha_{I}}\cdot\underbrace{\big(\min_{\theta\in[q]}(\mathbb{E}_{u}g^{2}_{u,J})(\theta)\big)^{1/2}}_{:=\beta_{J}},

for every pair
I,J\subseteq[d_{u}]
with
|I|\geq 2
and
|J|\geq 2
.

Using this inequality, (
88
) becomes

(91)

\displaystyle\big|(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta)-(\mathbb{E}_{u}f_{u}g_{u})(\theta^{\prime})\big|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{I,J\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:|I|,|J|\geq 2}a_{I,J}\alpha_{I}\beta_{J}=\vec{\alpha}^{\top}A\vec{\beta}\leq\|\vec{\alpha}\|\cdot\|A\|\cdot\|\vec{\beta}\|

where
\vec{\alpha}=(\alpha_{I})_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}
,

\vec{\beta}=(\beta)_{I\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:\,|I|\geq 2}
,
and

A=(a_{I,J})_{I,J\subseteq[d_{u}]\,:|I|,|J|\geq 2}
.
Further,
\|\vec{\alpha}\|
and
\|\vec{\beta}\|
are the
\ell_{2}
norms of
\vec{\alpha}
and
\vec{\beta}
, respectively, and
\|A\|
is the operator norm of
A
.

Estimate of operator norm of
A
:

Notice that
A
is a symmetric matrix.
Thus, we can fix a unit vector
\vec{\gamma}
satisfying

\|A\|=\vec{\gamma}^{\top}A\vec{\gamma}
.
For each pair
I,J\subseteq[d_{u}]
with
|I|\geq 2
and
|J|\geq 2
, since
a_{I,J}\geq 0
,

\gamma_{I}a_{I,J}\gamma_{J}\leq\frac{a_{I,J}}{2}\gamma_{I}^{2}+\frac{a_{I,J}}{2}\gamma_{J}^{2},

and thus,

(92)
